I didn’t have that sense that I was being punished, and I wasn’t hearing voices or anything like that. I have trouble falling asleep because I keep replaying phone calls and decisions about his care.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF33.3, F32.3, F33.2, F32.2, F43.23, F43.21\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I can’t shake this feeling—really this certainty—that I’m being punished for letting my father decline. My sister keeps showing me the doctor’s notes and telling me I did everything I could, that I didn’t do anything wrong, but it doesn’t matter. It still feels like some kind of judgment has already been passed on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe as a fact that you are being punished?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know it might not make sense to other people, but it feels like a fact to me. Even when my sister shows me the doctor’s notes and says I did everything right, I still feel certain that I’m being punished for what happened with my father.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, like everything has lost its color. It stays with me through the day. I can be sitting at my desk at work or looking at the garden beds, and it’s like none of it really reaches me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had the sense that ordinary events or messages are directed at you as part of this punishment?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do feel like things connect back to it. Not every little thing, but enough that it feels like reminders are being put in front of me. Even when my sister tries to show me the notes and says I did everything right, I still feel like the message is that I failed him and I’m being judged for it.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for days or weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve had hard periods before, like anyone, but I don’t know that I’ve ever felt this depressed for days or weeks in the same way as now.\n\nDoctor: Did these feelings start after your father became ill or died?\n\nPatient: Yes. It really started when my father’s health and behavior began changing, and I had to step in more as his caregiver. It’s been going on about a year and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function at work or take care of things day to day?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s definitely affected my day-to-day life. At work I’ve missed deadlines, and I’ve been avoiding the harder employee meetings that I normally would handle. And outside of work… I stopped showing up for my library volunteering because I just can’t keep myself steady enough to follow through.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt certain you were being punished even when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: No, not before this. I didn’t have that sense that I was being punished, and I wasn’t hearing voices or anything like that. That only started after I’d been feeling low and guilty and worn down for months, around when my father was declining.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping much more or much less than usual during this time?\n\nPatient: Much less than usual. I have trouble falling asleep because I keep replaying phone calls and decisions about his care. Then I wake up around 3 or 4 in the morning with my chest tight, and I usually can’t get back to sleep before work.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you are being punished because you caused your father’s decline?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know people keep telling me I didn’t cause it, and my sister has even shown me the doctor’s notes, but it doesn’t really change it. I still feel certain that I’m being punished for letting him decline… like some judgment has already been made against me.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, was there ever a time in your life when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… before this, I’ve had times where I felt depressed most of the day, nearly every day. It’s like I’d wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, and everything just seemed drained of color. I could be sitting at my desk at work, or looking at the garden beds, and it felt like none of it really reached me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Did one of those earlier periods of depression happen before your father became ill?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had depressed periods before, but I can’t clearly place whether one was before he got sick. The timing feels a little blurry to me.\n\nDoctor: Has it been more than six months since your father died?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I know it’s been a while, but I’m having trouble keeping the timing straight.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, was there any period when you still felt certain you were being punished even though your depressed mood had lifted?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. The feeling that I was being punished didn’t happen when my mood was okay. I never had that before all this started with my father getting worse. It came after months of feeling really low and guilty and worn down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a clear loss of appetite during this time?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed a clear loss of appetite, exactly. Some days I don’t feel like eating much, but I can’t say it’s been a definite change.\n\nDoctor: Do other people around you agree that you are being punished for your father’s decline?\n\nPatient: No. My sister doesn’t agree with me. She keeps showing me the doctor’s notes and telling me I did everything I could, that I didn’t cause his decline. But I still can’t shake this feeling that I’m being punished for it, like some kind of judgment has already been made against me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know it might not make sense to other people, but it feels like a fact to me. Even when my sister shows me the doctor’s notes and says I did everything right, I still feel certain that I’m being punished for what happened with my father.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, like everything has lost its color. It stays with me through the day. I’ve had hard periods before, like anyone, but I don’t know that I’ve ever felt this depressed for days or weeks in the same way as now.\n\nDoctor: Did these feelings start after your father became ill or died?\n\nPatient: Yes.
